<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the Hausdorff dimension of every finite real self-similar measure equals the minimum of one and its random-walk entropy rate divided by its Lyapunov exponent. Exact overlaps are allowed, and the contraction ratios may be unequal and negative. This resolves the entropy-rate dimension conjecture.

- **claim-entropy-rate-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every finite nonempty indexed family $\varphi_i(x)=r_i x+t_i$ on $\mathbb R$ with $0<\|r_i\|<1$, and every strictly positive probability vector $p$, its self-similar measure satisfies $$\dim_H\mu_{\Phi,p} =\min\left\{1,\frac{h_{\mathrm{RW}}(\Phi,p)}{\chi(\Phi,p)}\right\}.$$ No separation assumption is required; exact overlaps and repeated generators are allowed.
  - Explanation: The dimension is determined by the entropy rate of complete affine maps and the mean contraction depth. Colliding addresses are combined before entropy is measured, and signed unequal contractions are permitted.
- **claim-averaged-entropy-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-calculus)): For every compactly supported real law, $$\label{eq:entropy-smoothing} \mathcal H_s(X)=h(X+V_s)-\log s, \qquad \mathcal H_s(aX+b)=\mathcal H_{s/\|a\|}(X) \quad(a\ne0).$$ If $t/s=N$ is a positive integer, then $G_{s,t}$ is concave in the law, $$\label{eq:entropy-integer-window} 0\le G_{s,t}\le\log N, \qquad G_{s,t}(X+Y)\ge G_{s,t}(X)$$ for independent bounded $X,Y$. For arbitrary $z\ge s>0$, $$\label{eq:entropy-scale-comparison} \mathcal H_s-\log(z/s+2)\le\mathcal H_z\le\mathcal H_s+1.$$ Furthermore, $$\label{eq:entropy-grid-comparison} \|\mathcal H_s(X)-H_s^0(X)\|\le1, \qquad \|H_s^0(X+b)-H_s^0(X)\|\le1.$$
  - Explanation: Uniform smoothing represents averaged grid entropy. Signed affine changes rescale the grid, while nested grids give concavity and convolution monotonicity when the scale ratio is an integer. Grid comparison bounds control changes of scale and translation.
- **claim-entropy-dimension-upper** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-dimension)): Let $\nu$ be a compactly supported exact-dimensional probability measure on $\mathbb R$ of dimension $d$. Then $$\label{eq:entropy-dimension-upper} \limsup_{s\downarrow0} \frac{\max\{\mathcal H_s(\nu),H_s^0(\nu)\}} {\log(1/s)}\le d.$$
  - Explanation: Exact dimensionality bounds the asymptotic growth of both fixed-grid and averaged grid entropies. Compact support controls the entropy contribution outside a set where local mass estimates hold uniformly.
- **claim-uniform-nonsaturation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-nonsaturation)): Let $\mu$ be a self-similar probability measure for a finite affine system $x\mapsto r_i x+t_i$ with $0<\|r_i\|<1$. If $\mu$ is exact dimensional with dimension $d<1$, then $$\label{eq:uniform-nonsaturation} \delta:=\inf_{v>0}\Delta_v(\mu)>0.$$ No separation or orientation assumption is needed.
  - Explanation: A self-similar measure of dimension below the ambient dimension has a uniform positive entropy deficit across all positive scales. Stopping the coding transfers hypothetical near-saturation to smaller scales and contradicts the entropy dimension bound.
- **claim-fair-pair-entropy-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-point)): Let $X$ be bounded and $J$ an independent fair bit. For $0<s<v<t$, $$\label{eq:two-point-gain} G_{s,t}(X+vJ)-G_{s,t}(X) \ge\Delta_v(X)-s/v-v/t.$$ The same estimate holds for convolution with any fair two-point law whose two points have distance $v$.
  - Explanation: Convolution with a fair pair converts an entropy deficit at the pair distance into entropy gain across a wider window. The losses account for the fine and coarse boundary scales, without requiring an integer scale ratio.
- **claim-finite-law-pair-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pair-bound)): For every integer $k\ge1$ there is a finite constant $C_k$ such that every finitely supported probability law $\nu$ on $\mathbb R$ and every $\rho>0$ satisfy $$\label{eq:pair-bound} k\bigl(H(\nu)-H^0_\rho(\nu)\bigr) \le \log\|kF\| +C_k\sum_{\ell:\,2^{-\ell}\le2\rho}c_\ell(\nu), \qquad F=\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nu,$$ where $kF=\{x_1+\cdots+x_k:x_i\in F\}$. The constant is independent of the smallest positive distance in $F$.
  - Explanation: Information hidden within grid cells is bounded by sumset entropy and fair-pair capacities at finer scales. Randomized nested partitions prevent arbitrarily close atoms from causing a dependence on minimum support separation.
- **claim-type-conditioned-entropy-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-type-deficit)): For a system satisfying <a href="#eq:types-loss" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:types-loss">[eq:types-loss]</a>, with the parameters <a href="#eq:types-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:types-parameters">[eq:types-parameters]</a> and conditional laws <a href="#eq:types-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:types-laws">[eq:types-laws]</a>, for every $n\ge1$ and $s>0$ we have $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb EH(\nu_{\omega,n}) &=H(G_{bn}\mid\Omega_n) \ge nh_b,\label{eq:types-exact-entropy}\\ \mathbb EH_s^0(\nu_{\omega,n}) &\le H_s^0(\mu)+1.\label{eq:types-grid-entropy} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, there are $\varepsilon>0$ and an integer $n_0$ such that for every $n\ge n_0$, $$\label{eq:types-deficit} \mathbb E\!\left[\mathbf1_{E_n} \bigl(H(\nu_{\omega,n})-H_{\rho_n}^0(\nu_{\omega,n})\bigr) \right]\ge\varepsilon n.$$
  - Explanation: Conditioning on block counts fixes signed slopes, so conditional translation entropy equals conditional complete-map entropy. The count entropy is small enough that a hypothetical dimension deficit leaves a linear amount of entropy invisible to the selected grid.
- **claim-finite-pair-mass-bands** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pair-bands)): Under the assumptions and notation of Lemma <a href="#lem:type-deficit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:type-deficit">8</a>, there exist $c>0$ and an integer $n_0$ such that, for each $n\ge n_0$, there is a deterministic finite integer $B_n\ge a_n$ satisfying $$\label{eq:types-pair-bands} \mathbb E\!\left[\mathbf1_{E_n} \sum_{\ell=a_n}^{B_n}c_\ell(\nu_{\omega,n})\right]\ge cn.$$ Here $c_\ell(\nu)$ is the maximum total weight of fair two-point laws with separations in $[2^{-\ell},2^{1-\ell})$ whose weighted sum is a submeasure of $\nu$, as in Lemma <a href="#lem:pair-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pair-bound">7</a>. The constants $b,A,A',c,n_0$ are independent of $n$ and $\omega$; no growth bound on $B_n$ is asserted.
  - Explanation: The conditional deficit and a uniform sumset bound force linear expected pair mass. Finiteness of the conditional supports places this mass in a deterministic finite band, whose endpoint has no asserted growth bound.
- **claim-conditional-window-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-gain)): For all integers $j\ge0$, $n\ge1$, and $L\ge1$, with $g_j$ as in <a href="#eq:window-entropy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:window-entropy">[eq:window-entropy]</a>, $$\label{eq:conditional-gain} \mathbb E\bigl[g_j-g_{j+n}\mid\mathcal F_{j+n}\bigr] \ge \frac{\delta}{2} c_{L-\lceil\sigma_j\rceil}(\nu_{\theta^j\omega,n}).$$
  - Explanation: After conditioning through the prefix endpoint, the unobserved future averages to a scaled copy of the original measure. Convexity of the deficit then transfers uniform nonsaturation to the expected gain from a matched fair pair.
- **claim-disjoint-retained-windows** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-disjoint-windows)): For each fixed environment and target $L\in\{1,\ldots,T\}$, all the retained windows in <a href="#eq:retention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:retention">[eq:retention]</a> are pairwise disjoint. Consequently, $$\label{eq:telescoping-windows} \sum_{i=1}^q\sum_{j\in J_i} \mathbf1_{\mathcal A_{i,j,L}}(g_j-g_{j+n_i}) \le2\log M.$$
  - Explanation: Separated depth bands force retained windows from finer bands to end before coarser-band windows begin. Starts within a band are spaced by its block length, so all retained gains telescope within a common entropy budget.
- **claim-equal-length-overlap-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-overlap-lengths)): For a finite family of maps $x\mapsto r_i x+t_i$ with $0<\|r_i\|<1$, an equality of maps for two distinct nonempty words of arbitrary lengths implies such an equality for two distinct words of the same length.
  - Explanation: Invertibility and strict contraction exclude a proper-prefix collision. Concatenating the colliding words in opposite orders then yields distinct words of equal length defining the same map.
- **claim-no-overlap-measure-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-no-overlaps)): Let $\Phi$ be a finite nonempty indexed family of real similarities with $0<\|r_i\|<1$ and no exact overlaps. For every strictly positive probability vector $p$, $$\dim_H\mu_{\Phi,p}=\min\{1,H(p)/\chi(\Phi,p)\}.$$
  - Explanation: Without exact overlaps, complete maps identify their symbol words at each length. The random-walk entropy rate therefore equals the symbol entropy, giving the usual entropy-to-Lyapunov dimension formula.
- **claim-no-overlap-attractor-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-set)): For a finite nonempty family of similarities of $\mathbb R$ with arbitrary real translations and $0<\|r_i\|<1$, absence of exact overlaps implies $$\dim_H K_\Phi=\min\{1,s_*\}.$$
  - Explanation: The similarity weights produce a measure attaining the asserted lower dimension of the attractor. Cylinder coverings supply the matching upper bound, including the degenerate attractor case.
- **claim-homogeneous-all-weight-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-homogeneous)): Let $m\ge2$, $0<\lambda<1$, and $t_1,\ldots,t_m\in\mathbb R$. If the maps $x\mapsto\lambda x+t_i$ have no exact overlaps, then for every strictly positive probability vector $p$ their self-similar measure has $$\dim_H\mu=\min\left\{1,\frac{H(p)}{\log(1/\lambda)}\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: For a common positive contraction ratio, the Lyapunov exponent depends only on that ratio. The no-overlap measure formula therefore applies to every strictly positive choice of symbol weights.
- **claim-three-map-all-weight-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-three-maps)): Let $0<\lambda<1$ and $t\in\mathbb R$. If the indexed maps $$x\longmapsto\lambda x,\qquad x\longmapsto\lambda x+1,\qquad x\longmapsto\lambda x+t$$ have no exact overlaps, then for every $p_0,p_1,p_2>0$ with $p_0+p_1+p_2=1$, their self-similar measure satisfies $$\dim_H\mu =\min\left\{1,\frac{-\sum_{i=0}^2 p_i\log p_i} {\log(1/\lambda)}\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: The specified three-map family inherits the homogeneous formula for every positive probability vector, provided that distinct words do not define the same complete map.

If the indexed maps $$x\\longmapsto\\lambda x,\\qquad\n x\\longmapsto\\lambda x+1,\\qquad\n x\\longmapsto\\lambda x+t$$ have no exact overlaps, then for every $p_0,p_1,p_2>0$ with $p_0+p_1+p_2=1$, their self-similar measure satisfies $$\\dim_H\\mu\n =\\min\\left\\{1,\\frac{-\\sum_{i=0}^2 p_i\\log p_i}\n                         {\\log(1/\\lambda)}\\right\\}.$$",
      "explanation": "The specified three-map family inherits the homogeneous formula for every positive probability vector, provided that distinct words do not define the same complete map.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {

}

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finite indexed real affine contraction system** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty alphabet, and let $$\Phi=(\varphi_i)_{i\in\Lambda},\qquad \varphi_i(x)=r_i x+t_i,\qquad t_i\in\mathbb R,\quad 0<\|r_i\|<1.$$
- **Self-similar measure** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Given a probability vector $p=(p_i)_{i\in\Lambda}$ with $p_i>0$, its self-similar measure is the unique Borel probability measure satisfying $$\mu=\sum_{i\in\Lambda}p_i(\varphi_i)_*\mu.$$
- **Exact overlap** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An *exact overlap* is an equality $\varphi_u=\varphi_v$ for distinct words of the same positive length.
- **Complete-map random walk** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Set $$G_n=\varphi_{I_1}\circ\cdots\circ\varphi_{I_n},\qquad \mathbb P(G_n=g)=\sum_{w\in\Lambda^n:\,\varphi_w=g}p_w.$$
- **Random-walk entropy rate and Lyapunov exponent** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define the random-walk entropy rate and the Lyapunov exponent by $$\label{eq:rate-and-lyapunov} h=h_{\mathrm{RW}}(\Phi,p) :=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{H(G_n)}n =\inf_{n\ge1}\frac{H(G_n)}n, \qquad \chi=-\sum_i p_i\log\|r_i\|>0.$$
- **Lower Hausdorff dimension of a measure** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We use the lower Hausdorff dimension of a measure: $$\dim_H\nu=\inf\{\dim_H E:E\subset\mathbb R\text{ Borel},\ \nu(E)>0\}.$$
- **Fixed-grid and translation-averaged grid entropy** ([source](#sec:entropy)): For a bounded real random variable $X$ and $s>0$, define $$H_s^0(X)=H(\lfloor X/s\rfloor),\qquad \mathcal H_s(X)=\int_0^1H(\lfloor X/s+u\rfloor)\,du.$$
- **Entropy window and one-bit deficit** ([source](#sec:entropy)): For $s,t>0$, put $$G_{s,t}=\mathcal H_s-\mathcal H_t, \qquad \Delta_s=1-G_{s,2s}.$$
- **Pair-capacity distance band** ([source](#stmt-def-pair-capacity)): For $\ell\in\mathbb Z$, let $\mathcal E_\ell(F)$ be the set of unordered pairs $\{x,y\}\subset F$ satisfying $2^{-\ell}\le \|x-y\|<2^{1-\ell}$.
- **Fair-pair capacity** ([source](#stmt-def-pair-capacity)): Define $c_\ell(\nu)$ as the maximum of $$\sum_{e\in\mathcal E_\ell(F)}w_e \quad\text{subject to}\quad w_e\ge0,\qquad \frac12\sum_{e\ni x}w_e\le\nu(\{x\})\quad(x\in F).$$
- **Block type** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For $w\in\Lambda^b$ let $\operatorname{type}(w)=(\kappa_i)_{i\in\Lambda}$, where $\kappa_i$ counts the occurrences of $i$ in $w$.
- **Conditional prefix and infinite coding laws** ([source](#eq:types-laws)): Given $\omega$, use the independent conditional block draws above to define $$\label{eq:types-laws} \nu_{\omega,n} =\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}_{\omega}\!\left(\sum_{h=0}^{n-1} R_h\varphi_{W_h}(0)\right), \qquad \mu_\omega =\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}_{\omega}\!\left(\sum_{h=0}^{\infty} R_h\varphi_{W_h}(0)\right).$$
- **Signed dilation of a law** ([source](#eq:types-laws)): For any real $u\ne0$ and any law $\lambda$, write $\mathsf D_u\lambda=\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(uX)$ when $X$ has law $\lambda$; this notation retains the sign of $u$.
- **Similarity dimension** ([source](#sec:corollaries)): Its similarity dimension $s_*$ is the unique number $s_*\ge0$ satisfying $$\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\|r_i\|^{s_*}=1.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-measure-dimension | Hausdorff dimension of the self-similar measure | = | $\min\left\{1,\frac{h_{\mathrm{RW}}(\Phi,p)}{\chi(\Phi,p)}\right\}$ | The entropy rate counts complete maps, including all exact collisions. |
| q-integer-window-upper | Averaged entropy window increment | ≤ | $\log N$ | The bound and concavity use a positive integer scale ratio. |
| q-grid-averaging-error | Absolute difference between averaged and fixed-grid entropy | ≤ | $1$ | The comparison is uniform in the location of the grid. |
| q-uniform-deficit-positive | Infimum of the one-bit entropy deficit over positive scales | > | $0$ | The positive deficit depends on the measure; no numerical value is supplied. |
| q-two-point-window-gain | Increase of the averaged entropy window under fair-pair convolution | ≥ | $\Delta_v(X)-s/v-v/t$ | No integer scale-ratio condition is required. |
| q-explicit-pair-bound-coefficient | Admissible finite-law pair-bound constant | = | $60000k^4$ | This is an admissible choice, without a claim of optimality or dependence on support separation. |
| q-type-entropy-deficit | Expected conditional entropy deficit restricted to the contraction event | ≥ | $\varepsilon n$ | The constants and the selected scale are fixed by the preceding parameter choices. |
| q-pair-band-mass | Expected total pair capacity in a finite depth band on the contraction event | ≥ | $cn$ | The endpoint is finite for every relevant block count, with no asserted growth bound. |
| q-conditional-pair-gain | Conditional expected entropy gain across a block window | ≥ | $\frac{\delta}{2} c_{L-\lceil\sigma_j\rceil}(\nu_{\theta^j\omega,n})$ | Conditioning includes the types through the block endpoint and leaves future types unobserved. |
| q-retained-window-budget | Sum of entropy gains over all retained windows at a fixed target | ≤ | $2\log M$ | The bound is pathwise and uses the monotonicity of the suffix entropy windows. |
| q-no-overlap-attractor-dimension | Hausdorff dimension of the compact attractor | = | $\min\{1,s_*\}$ | Similarity dimension is defined by the contraction-ratio equation. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-map-entropy-upper-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:upper): Block the coding into distinct complete maps, apply the strong law to prefix probabilities and contractions, and cover the resulting full-measure coding images. -> The entropy-rate upper bound and the dimension-deficit contradiction hypothesis.
- **step-averaged-entropy-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-calculus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-calculus): Represent averaged entropy through uniform smoothing, use nested-grid conditional entropy for integer ratios, and compare grid intersections for the remaining bounds. -> Signed affine scaling, integer-ratio concavity and convolution monotonicity, and grid comparison inequalities.
- **step-entropy-dimension-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-dimension): Restrict to a large-mass set with uniform local lower mass bounds, count occupied cells using disjoint balls, and bound the entropy of the remaining compactly supported mass. -> The fine-scale entropy growth upper bound supplied by dimension.
- **step-uniform-nonsaturation** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonsaturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonsaturation): Convert small deficit into closeness of a smoothed law and its translate, obtain wider-window saturation, and transfer it to all smaller scales by stopped self-similarity. -> A strictly positive uniform one-bit entropy deficit.
- **step-two-point-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-point): Express pair-convolution entropy changes as information about the fair label, compare fine observations, and bound the coarse observation by its boundary-crossing probability. -> The entropy-gain lower bound for any fair pair at the specified distance.
- **step-finite-law-pair-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-pair-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-bound): Reveal the sum of independent samples, refine their initial grid cells through randomized nested partitions, charge exceptional label changes to symmetric pair couplings, and sum geometrically across finer levels. -> An entropy-deficit bound using sumset cardinality and total fine-scale pair capacity, independent of minimum separation.
- **step-type-conditioned-deficit** [claim](#stmt-lem-type-deficit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-type-deficit): Condition on block symbol counts, identify complete maps with translations at fixed signed slopes, bound the count entropy, compare grid entropy with the original measure, and discard atypical contraction paths. -> Linear expected hidden entropy on the typical contraction event.
- **step-localize-pair-mass** [claim](#stmt-prop-pair-bands) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pair-bands): Bound conditional sumsets by counting block-digit multiplicities, apply the finite-law pair bound, and collect the finitely many nonzero capacity bands across all type prefixes. -> A linear expected amount of pair mass in a deterministic finite band.
- **step-average-future-pair-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-gain): Fix a maximizing pair decomposition after conditioning through the block endpoint, match pair distances to the observation scale, and average future types before applying deficit convexity and nonsaturation. -> The conditional expected gain proportional to matched pair capacity.
- **step-disjoint-retained-windows** [claim](#stmt-lem-disjoint-windows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-disjoint-windows): Choose successive block lengths after earlier band endpoints are known, retain windows satisfying the contraction and target-band conditions, and use monotone adjusted depths to order all retained windows. -> Pairwise disjoint retained windows and the pathwise telescoping budget.
- **step-complete-dimension-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-disjoint-windows-2): Sum conditional gains across target depths, recover every pair scale in each retained band, and compare the fixed entropy budget with the contribution of an arbitrary number of bands. -> The dimension-deficit hypothesis is impossible, proving the entropy-rate dimension equality.
- **step-equal-length-overlap-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-overlap-lengths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-overlap-lengths): Exclude a proper-prefix collision by invertibility and strict contraction, then concatenate the colliding words in opposite orders. -> Distinct equal-length words defining the same complete map.
- **step-no-overlap-measure-formula** [claim](#stmt-cor-no-overlaps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-no-overlaps): Use injectivity of the word-to-map assignment at each length to identify the random-walk entropy rate with symbol entropy, then apply the main theorem. -> The no-overlap measure dimension formula.
- **step-attractor-formula** [claim](#stmt-cor-set) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-set): Choose weights given by the similarity dimension to obtain the lower bound from a supported measure, and use cylinder coverings for the upper bound. -> The attractor Hausdorff dimension equals the ambient cap of similarity dimension.
- **step-homogeneous-formula** [claim](#stmt-cor-homogeneous) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-homogeneous): Specialize all contraction ratios to the common positive ratio and simplify the Lyapunov exponent. -> The homogeneous all-weight measure dimension formula.
- **step-three-map-formula** [claim](#stmt-cor-three-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-three-maps): Insert the specified translations into the homogeneous all-weight formula. -> The three-map entropy dimension formula.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-map-entropy-dimension**: The dimension formula uses the entropy rate of complete affine maps, with exact collisions combined, and permits signed unequal contractions and repeated generators.
- **invariant-average-unobserved-future**: The nonsaturation estimate applies after averaging the unobserved future; it is not assumed for each conditional tail separately.
- **invariant-finite-bands-without-growth-control**: The pair-mass bands have finite deterministic endpoints, but their existence supplies no asserted growth bound for those endpoints.
- **invariant-disjoint-window-budget**: Within each band the candidate windows do not overlap, and the separated-band construction also orders windows from different bands before telescoping their entropy gains.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-dimension-as-density**: Treat full Hausdorff dimension as a certificate of absolute continuity or density regularity. -> The dimension conclusion does not establish absolute continuity.
- **anti-use-address-entropy-under-collisions**: Replace map entropy by symbol entropy even when distinct addresses define the same affine map. -> Address entropy retains distinctions that the map walk has already lost.
- **anti-use-uniform-endpoint-runtime**: Turn finite existence of the pair-band endpoints into a prescribed termination bound for numerical dimension estimation. -> The proof permits endpoints beyond any supplied effective bound and does not provide a quantitative convergence rate.
- **anti-use-pathwise-tail-nonsaturation**: Apply the original measure's nonsaturation floor directly to every environment-conditioned tail. -> An individual conditional tail need not inherit that floor.
- **anti-use-correlated-driving**: Apply the formula unchanged to a correlated or state-dependent sequence of generators. -> Such a driving process is outside the independent coding law used to define the measure and map walk.
- **anti-use-general-dynamics**: Transfer the theorem directly to nonlinear transformations, noncontracting maps, or systems outside the real line. -> The stated theorem supplies no dimension identity for those systems.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The selected result is the Hausdorff dimension identity for an infinite self-similar measure, obtained through asymptotic entropy rates and a contradiction over successively chosen scale bands. Finite pair-capacity constructions are subsidiary objects; testing them would not decide the dimension identity. The source explicitly states: "The constants $b,A,A',c,n_0$ are independent of $n$ and $\omega$; no growth bound on $B_n$ is asserted." Thus the proof does not supply a finite algorithm with a certified stopping rule for the selected result.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite complete-map distribution gives a concrete contract for aggregating probabilities of coding words that produce identical affine maps. This supports finite-prefix analysis when map equality and probability arithmetic have exact representations. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The dimension formula can inform the analysis of real affine fractal models, but it supplies no rendering, collision-handling, or simulation accuracy contract. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The overlap-length proof constructs an equal-length collision witness from an already established collision between distinct nonempty words. This can normalize witnesses produced by an exact affine-composition checker. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Pair capacity is a finite optimization construction: enumerate support pairs in a specified distance band and maximize their total admissible fair-pair weight. Exact implementation requires decidable distance comparisons for the represented support. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Conditioning on block types identifies translations with complete maps, but the paper provides no storage layout, indexing scheme, or data-structure performance guarantee. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The randomized partition refinement is a proof device for an entropy estimate. It does not specify an accelerator architecture, a parallel execution schedule, or a finite precision implementation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Random-walk Shannon entropy measures uncertainty of composed affine maps. The paper supplies no adversarial model, computational hardness assumption, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coding construction assumes independent symbols with a fixed probability law. It supplies no learning rule or guarantee for trained, correlated, or state-dependent generators. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The pair-capacity constraints define a fractional packing problem: support masses are vertex budgets, eligible fair pairs are resources consuming equal mass at their endpoints, and the objective maximizes packed mass. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Disjoint retained windows prevent repeated counting of entropy gains along a conditional coding sequence. They are not a distributed scheduling, communication, or fault-tolerance construction. |
| other (Geometric measure theory and dynamical systems) | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper directly determines the dimension of finite real self-similar measures, including systems with exact collisions. Its limiting entropy rate and proof bands do not furnish a general finite dimension-evaluation procedure. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-finite-real-affine-contractions** (formal_scope): The dimension identity concerns finite nonempty indexed affine contraction systems on the real line. Extensions to nonlinear maps, infinite dictionaries, or other ambient spaces are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Apply the dimension formula to a nonlinear generator.', 'Apply the formula to an infinite family or to maps outside the stated contraction range.']
- **limit-positive-independent-driving** (hypothesis): The stated measure uses strictly positive probabilities and independent symbols with a fixed law. A corresponding result for dependent driving is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Transfer the identity directly to a correlated or state-dependent generator.', 'Retain zero-weight indexed generators while asserting that the stated positive-weight hypothesis holds.']
- **limit-complete-map-equality** (formal_scope): Exact overlap requires equality of complete affine maps, including signed slope and translation. Translation agreement alone or numerical proximity does not establish the required equality. Blocked use: ['Merge maps using translations alone when their signed slopes differ.', 'Treat a numerical tolerance as proof of an exact overlap.']
- **limit-address-entropy-substitution** (hypothesis): The general formula uses complete-map entropy after collisions have been combined. Substitution of symbol entropy requires the no-exact-overlap hypothesis used in the corollary. Blocked use: ['Use symbol entropy as the random-walk entropy rate in a system with exact map collisions.', 'Count repeated indexed generators as distinct outcomes of the complete-map walk.']
- **limit-integer-ratio-concavity** (hypothesis): The window concavity and convolution-monotonicity argument uses an integer ratio of scales. The general scale-comparison inequalities do not remove that condition. Blocked use: ['Invoke window concavity at an arbitrary noninteger scale ratio.', 'Transfer the nested-grid convolution argument without checking its ratio hypothesis.']
- **limit-entropy-dimension-assumptions** (hypothesis): The entropy growth comparison assumes both compact support and exact dimensionality. It is an asymptotic upper bound, rather than a finite-scale identity. Blocked use: ['Apply the entropy comparison to an arbitrary unbounded probability law.', 'Replace the asymptotic upper bound by equality at a chosen observation scale.']
- **limit-nonsaturation-regime** (hypothesis): Uniform positive nonsaturation is established for an exact-dimensional self-similar measure whose dimension is below the ambient cap. The lemma does not supply that positive floor for a full-dimensional measure. Blocked use: ['Assume a positive uniform deficit for a full-dimensional measure.', 'Apply the self-similar nonsaturation conclusion to an arbitrary compactly supported law.']
- **limit-fair-pair-gain-preconditions** (hypothesis): The pair-gain estimate requires a bounded variable, an independent fair bit, and strictly ordered fine, pair, and coarse scales. An unequal-weight pair is not covered by that estimate as stated. Blocked use: ['Apply the same gain bound to a biased pair without another argument.', 'Use the gain estimate when the pair distance is outside its observation window.']
- **limit-finite-law-pair-bound** (hypothesis): The pair bound is stated for finite support and includes a sumset term. It does not justify dropping that term or passing to an infinite support without an additional argument. Blocked use: ['Infer the pair bound for an infinite-support law from its finite-support statement.', 'Deduce a lower bound on pair mass without controlling the sumset contribution.']
- **limit-pair-capacity-optimality** (formal_scope): Pair capacity is a maximum over admissible fractional pair weights. A feasible decomposition supplies a lower bound on capacity, not automatically its value; an empty eligible band has zero capacity. Blocked use: ['Report the weight of an arbitrary feasible pair packing as the maximum capacity.', 'Impose an integral matching restriction while claiming to compute the defined capacity.']
- **limit-type-deficit-contradiction-regime** (hypothesis): The linear conditional deficit and resulting pair bands are derived under the dimension-loss contradiction hypothesis. They are not unconditional positive-deficit conclusions for every self-similar system. Blocked use: ['Assert positive linear hidden entropy for a system already attaining the dimension formula.', 'Reuse the pair-band conclusion without its preceding parameter and conditional-law construction.']
- **limit-conditioning-preserves-signed-slopes** (formal_scope): Translation identifies a complete composed map only after conditioning fixes its signed slope. This conditional identification does not permit discarding slopes from the unrestricted map walk. Blocked use: ['Identify maps globally by translation while contraction products vary.', 'Replace signed contraction products by their absolute values in conditional map identities.']
- **limit-average-unobserved-tail** (hypothesis): The conditional gain uses averaging over future types after conditioning through the segment endpoint. A pathwise nonsaturation bound for each fully conditioned tail is not established. Blocked use: ["Apply the original measure's positive deficit directly to every conditional tail.", 'Condition on the entire future before using the stated conditional-gain argument.']
- **limit-retained-window-selection** (hypothesis): The telescoping budget applies to retained windows selected by the prescribed separation, candidate-start, and retention construction. Arbitrary overlapping windows need not satisfy that budget. Blocked use: ['Sum entropy gains from overlapping block intervals as if they were disjoint.', 'Use independently chosen depth bands without verifying the separated-band condition.']
- **limit-finite-endpoints-without-effective-bound** (nonconstructive): Finite deterministic pair-band endpoints are asserted without a growth bound. This lack of a bound does not itself prove that the endpoints exceed every effective bound. Blocked use: ['Extract a prescribed runtime or stopping depth from endpoint finiteness alone.', 'Assert an impossibility theorem for all effective endpoint bounds from the absence of an asserted bound.']
- **limit-no-certified-convergence-rate** (approximation): The dimension statements supply no quantitative convergence rate or effective separation guarantee. Finite-prefix entropy calculations do not acquire a certified approximation error from this theorem alone. Blocked use: ['Treat finite-prefix map entropy as an exact evaluation of its limiting rate.', 'Attach a guaranteed dimension-approximation error to a chosen finite depth without additional estimates.']
- **limit-dimension-does-not-certify-density** (formal_scope): The conclusion determines Hausdorff dimension without asserting absolute continuity or density regularity, including at critical and supercritical entropy rates. Blocked use: ['Use full Hausdorff dimension as a certificate of absolute continuity.', 'Infer a density or its regularity from the dimension identity.']
- **limit-no-overlap-corollaries** (hypothesis): The symbol-entropy and similarity-dimension corollaries require absence of exact overlaps. Allowing overlaps in the main theorem does not remove that condition from these specializations. Blocked use: ['Apply the similarity-dimension attractor equality to an overlapping system solely because the main measure theorem allows overlaps.', 'Use the homogeneous symbol-entropy formula without establishing its no-overlap hypothesis.']
- **limit-overlap-witness-not-global-decision** (formal_scope): The overlap-length argument transforms a supplied collision witness. It supplies no finite search cutoff for deciding absence of every exact overlap, and its cancellation argument uses invertibility and strict contraction. Blocked use: ['Claim absence of all exact overlaps after an arbitrary bounded word search.', 'Apply the prefix-cancellation construction to a noninvertible generator.']
- **limit-trivial-entropy-case** (edge_case): The entropy-rate upper bound already settles the vanishing-entropy case, including a single-map system. The positive-deficit contradiction construction is unnecessary there. Blocked use: ['Force the positive-entropy block-parameter construction onto a single-map system.', 'Treat the vanishing-entropy case as an unresolved exception.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 148 (`lean/docs/148.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The entropy-rate dimension formula for self-similar measures on the line](../../preprints/The-entropy-rate-dimension-formula-for-self-similar-measures-on-the-line-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves $\dim_H\mu=\min\{1,h_{\mathrm{RW}}/\chi\}$ for the lower Hausdorff dimension of every finite real self-similar probability measure with positive weights and nonzero contraction ratios of absolute value below one. Here $h_{\mathrm{RW}}$ is the entropy rate of random affine compositions and $\chi$ is the corresponding Lyapunov exponent in the same logarithmic base. Ratios may be signed and unequal, and exact overlaps are allowed.
>
> It also gives two consequences without exact overlaps: the usual entropy-over-Lyapunov formula for a common positive contraction ratio, and the attractor formula $\dim_H K=\min\{1,s\}$, where $s\ge0$ is the unique solution of $\sum_i|r_i|^s=1$. Absolute continuity is outside these statements.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

universe uι

open scoped BigOperators ENNReal
open MeasureTheory

namespace EntropyRateDimension

structure System (ι : Type uι) [Fintype ι] where
  ratio : ι → ℝ
  offset : ι → ℝ
  weight : ι → ℝ
  ratio_pos : ∀ i, 0 < |ratio i|
  ratio_lt_one : ∀ i, |ratio i| < 1
  weight_pos : ∀ i, 0 < weight i
  weight_sum : ∑ i, weight i = 1

namespace System

variable {ι : Type uι} [Fintype ι]

def affine (S : System ι) (i : ι) (x : ℝ) : ℝ := S.ratio i * x + S.offset i

def wordAffine (S : System ι) : List ι → ℝ × ℝ
  | [] => (1, 0)
  | i :: w => (S.ratio i * (S.wordAffine w).1,
      S.ratio i * (S.wordAffine w).2 + S.offset i)

def completeMap (S : System ι) {n : ℕ} (w : Fin n → ι) : ℝ × ℝ :=
  S.wordAffine (List.ofFn w)

noncomputable def wordWeight (S : System ι) {n : ℕ} (w : Fin n → ι) : ℝ :=
  ∏ j, S.weight (w j)

noncomputable def mapSupport (S : System ι) (n : ℕ) : Finset (ℝ × ℝ) := by
  classical
  exact Finset.univ.image (S.completeMap (n := n))

noncomputable def mapMass (S : System ι) (n : ℕ) (g : ℝ × ℝ) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact ∑ w ∈ (Finset.univ : Finset (Fin n → ι)).filter (S.completeMap · = g),
    S.wordWeight w

noncomputable def walkEntropy (S : System ι) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (∑ g ∈ S.mapSupport n, Real.negMulLog (S.mapMass n g)) / Real.log 2

noncomputable def entropyRate (S : System ι) : ℝ :=
  sInf (Set.range fun n : ℕ => S.walkEntropy (n + 1) / (n + 1 : ℕ))

noncomputable def lyapunov (S : System ι) : ℝ :=
  -(∑ i, S.weight i * (Real.log |S.ratio i| / Real.log 2))

def SelfSimilar (S : System ι) (μ : Measure ℝ) : Prop :=
  μ = ∑ i, ENNReal.ofReal (S.weight i) • μ.map (S.affine i)

end System

noncomputable def lowerHausdorffDimension (μ : Measure ℝ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨅ (E : Set ℝ) (_ : MeasurableSet E) (_ : 0 < μ E), dimH E
open MeasureTheory

theorem entropy_rate_dimension {ι : Type uι} [Fintype ι] [Nonempty ι]
    (S : System ι) (μ : Measure ℝ) [IsProbabilityMeasure μ]
    (hμ : S.SelfSimilar μ) :
    lowerHausdorffDimension μ = ENNReal.ofReal (min 1 (S.entropyRate / S.lyapunov)) := by
  sorry

end EntropyRateDimension

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the Hausdorff dimension of every finite real self-similar measure equals the minimum of one and its random-walk entropy rate divided by its Lyapunov exponent. Exact overlaps are allowed, and the contraction ratios may be unequal and negative. This resolves the entropy-rate dimension conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
